## 2026-10-30 - Claude - Live service graph dashboard working and ported into the repo

**Request:** User shared a Codex transcript in which Tempo plus a service-graph dashboard were hand-edited directly in `/opt/navio` on the VM. The node graph showed "No data", the error table was empty, and edges such as `user -> trip-planning-service` were misleading. User asked for a working "which request goes to which backend" tree that looks good.
**Findings:** (1) `deploy.sh` reinstalls compose, observability configs and dashboards from the repo on every release, so the VM-only Tempo edits would revert to Zipkin on the next deploy. (2) The node graph needs the Tempo datasource's Service Graph query (`queryType: serviceMap`, with `serviceMap.datasourceUid: prometheus`); a plain Prometheus query cannot feed it. (3) The `user -> <service>` edges at about 0.01 req/s are Prometheus scrapes and Docker health checks hitting `/actuator`, each starting its own trace. They were not missing gateway propagation.
**Done (root, uncommitted):** Services export Zipkin to `alloy:9411`. Alloy's new `otelcol` pipeline drops `/actuator` spans and sends OTLP to Tempo. New `.deploy/observability/tempo.yml` (Tempo 2.8.2: service-graphs and span-metrics remote-write to Prometheus, 24h local traces). Compose: Zipkin replaced by `tempo` (384m, GOMEMLIMIT 300MiB, healthcheck disabled like Zipkin so it never gates a release, `tempo-data` volume); Prometheus gets `--web.enable-remote-write-receiver`. Grafana datasource Zipkin replaced by Tempo (uid `tempo`), and Loki trace links now point to Tempo. Prometheus scrapes `tempo:3200`. `deploy.sh` installs `tempo.yml`. New `grafana/dashboards/navio-service-graph.json` (uid `navio-service-graph`, so the same URL): stat row, a layered node graph "Request flow", "Calls between services" and "Endpoints by backend" tables (req/s, failed %, p95), and per-edge rate and failure charts. An "Infrastructure calls" Hide/Show variable filters Eureka and config-server edges. Its generator is scratch-only; edit the JSON directly. README and `check-observability.sh` updated from Zipkin to Tempo, including a service-graph-edges probe; the memory ceiling is now 9,168 MiB.
**Verified:** Local Docker stack running the exact repo configs (Prometheus 3.14.0, Tempo 2.8.2, Alloy 1.12.2, Grafana 12.3.2) with synthetic Spring-shaped Zipkin spans: gateway, nested service calls, Kafka, failures, actuator probes and Eureka heartbeats. Actuator spans never reached Tempo, and the only entry edge was `user -> api-gateway`. Screenshots from real-time headless Chrome over CDP: the node graph renders the tree in both Hide and Show modes, and the tables and stats fill. `docker compose config` on production compose passes with dummy env. `bash -n` passes on `deploy.sh` and `check-observability.sh`. Test stack removed. Docker Desktop was started for this and left running. Not checked against real production traffic.
**Not done / next:** Not deployed. Root is still mid-merge (docs/api, docs/database conflicts from another session), so nothing was committed. To ship it, resolve that merge, commit on a `feat/`/`chore/` branch -> dev -> main (a release). Or hot-apply by copying the 6 changed config/dashboard files to `/opt/navio` and running `docker compose up -d`, which recreates the Java services because their tracing endpoint changed. On the VM, the Codex-created `navio-tempo-data` volume becomes unused after this. Grafana runs `navio-service-graph` from this file instead of the Codex version.

## 2026-10-13 - Codex - Diagnose Google Maps versus Tokyo transit API

**Goal:** Answer whether Navio can show Google Maps-style transit alternatives after the user supplied a Tokyo Tower to New National Theatre example that is available in Google Maps but unverified in Navio.
**Done:** Read current provider/proxy code and verified official Google documentation. Public read-only routing requests through both localhost:3000/api/routes/directions and localhost:8080/v1/routes/directions returned transit fallback, reason No public transit route was found, 6012 m direct distance and no steps. Google Maps Platform FAQ explicitly states Routes API excludes Japanese transit partners (also IRCTC). Therefore the Tokyo example has a provider coverage limitation, not simply a missing UI. Google Routes can return up to three alternatives in addition to the main route, station and departure/arrival details, and fares where supplied, in supported regions. Current Navio keeps one provider route, requests alternatives only for rail-only searches, strips schedule metadata, and reports moving time rather than complete scheduled journey time. Google-style route rows need a richer provider/response contract. NAVITIME's official transit API is a viable Japan-capable integration option with separate access and relevant timetable/service options to check.
**Sources:** https://developers.google.com/maps/faq#transit_directions_countries ; https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/routes/transit-route ; https://api-sdk.navitime.co.jp/api/specs/api_guide/route_transit.html
**Verified:** Current source inspection, official documentation and two live Navio public routing responses. No account/planner writes, new provider account/API key, application edits, restart, schema changes, push or deployment. Prior travel UI remains uncommitted on existing branches. Concurrent auth/guest-removal and development-runner changes were observed and preserved; prior frontend test counts are historical for their tested state.
**Follow-ups:** Extend the common transit journey contract to include alternatives, schedule context, overall elapsed time, walking, lines/stops and nullable fares. Keep Google for covered regions and integrate a licensed Japan-capable transit source for Tokyo; changing the UI or departure time alone cannot overcome the documented Japan exclusion. No new branches, per the user's instruction. Session notes committed on the existing root docs branch and fast-forwarded locally into dev only.

Search/finder no longer treat OTHER as compatible; active known shared connectors and usable power are required for charging. Unknown/stale/busy availability generates a warning. ## 2026-10-05 - Codex - Route-loading request-limit correction

**Authority/scope:** User approved the focused route-loading fix after diagnosis, before Phase 4B. No UI, energy formula, backend, billing, optimizer or schema changes. No push/deployment requested for this correction.

**Diagnosis evidence:** The connected localhost proxy returned HTTP 400 for a synthetic 11-group request: validationErrors.groups = size must be between 1 and 10. Mobility also limits each group to 25 points. The previous client sent all groups/points together. The original user's specific failed request was not captured, so this is a reproduced defect, not proof that every route failure has this cause. Old error parsing ignored message/validationErrors when error was null.

**Implementation:** Client fix/route-request-batching adds trip-route-api.ts, imported/re-exported by trip-route-query.ts. Splits long days into <=25-point chunks with one shared endpoint, packs <=10 groups per request sequentially, and preserves block/point IDs, order, profile and fallback/null data. Only returns the complete combined response; failed, malformed or mismatched/incomplete batches reject instead of becoming partial route data. TanStack cancellation signal stops further batches when obsolete. HTTP status is logged in text; message/error/validationErrors are retained rather than hidden by the console overlay.

**Verification:** 127 frontend tests passed including eight new route tests (25 days, 2/24/25/26/49/50/242 points, start/end anchors, later batch failure, proxy/non-JSON error, malformed/missing/mismatched legs, fallback null duration, cancellation/empty input). Initial TypeScript error in unknown-payload narrowing corrected; TypeScript, targeted ESLint and affected eight tests then passed. git diff --check passed. No new production build or live Google routing acceptance; no backend changes to retest. Original local tsconfig/CLAUDE/log/report files preserved.

**Next:** Recheck the affected real trip in the local app. Further failures should expose the actual HTTP/backend reason. Google Maps billing remains a separate unresolved Cloud Console issue. Phase 4B/C remain outstanding. This correction is local; root submodule pins remain at the published Phase 4A baseline until an authorized publication.

**Reinspection findings (no application changes):** Frontend EvRouteOptimizationPanel keys preview only by request payload and reads the saved snapshot without explicitly flushing pending local edits. Apply passes expectedVersion; TripEvOptimizationService enforces ownership/version and recomputes feasibility, but the inspected apply path does not independently enforce consumption provenance/rated-range automatic-apply eligibility or resolve the model from the saved trip snapshot. TripEvOptimizationApplier stores estimated charge duration rounded to an integer; selected target/continuous-state parity remains a Phase 4 concern. Backend optimizer and road verifier already use CanonicalEnergy: extend these rather than recreate them. Optimization UI currently requires authenticated saved trips. trip-energy-projection.ts suppresses the route-chart profile on observed checkpoints/missing states; numerical chronological propagation still exists. Current settings no longer expose the old standalone legacy-confirm button, while helper copy still refers to confirmation: resolve that mismatch deliberately, without relabelling unknown values as user observations.

**Recommended next work:**
1. Baseline acceptance: preserve local work before pulling root dev and recursively updating pins. Browser-test guest/signed-in catalogue/custom flows, settings, trip-only membership/reusable custom list, first-day SoC/reopen, multiple checkpoints/clear, shared read-only views and copy privacy. Adapt the existing browser test selectors to the new UI; verify frontend and backend capability versions together. Inspect Flyway history before release: published V11/V13 remain intact, energy V14 and garage V15 are now allocated. Never renumber these again or blindly repair environments that previously ran the old checkpoint V11/V12. The disposable published-schema upgrade test passes, but is not production-history verification.
2. Phase 4A: bind optimizer inputs to canonical persisted trip snapshot/version and preceding chronological state. Enforce model eligibility on server as well as frontend; separate physical reachability, reserve sufficiency and automatic-apply policy. Retain currently approved rated-range preview-only and explicit legacy handling unless maintainer intentionally revises the contract. No hidden standard scaling, inferred usable capacity, or duplicated margin. Keep observation, charging and consumption override distinct.
3. Phase 4B: strengthen existing routed verifier and bounded search for first-charger access, all-leg reserve, detours/anchors, unreachable routes, missing route data, connector/power uncertainty, locked stops, multiple days/checkpoints and zero/negative state. Confirm target/power/duration semantics and limitations of constant-power nominal charging. Do not call bounded search globally optimal or unknown charger availability confirmed available.
4. Phase 4C: flush pending edits before preview; invalidate on every relevant itinerary/model/SoC/checkpoint/reserve change. Retain ownership/optimistic locking and revalidation. Persist charging targets and sufficient precision so preview, apply and reload agree, with atomic failures. Decide guest optimization scope explicitly: current guest manual planning/catalogue/custom is supported, account-backed optimizer is authenticated.
5. Phase 4 validation: shared TS/Java fixtures, split-leg invariance, unknown-versus-zero, usable-capacity missing, unreachable prior leg followed by observation, checkpoint removal/multiple days, stale/concurrent preview/apply, target reload, privacy/ownership and trip separation. Repeat touched suites and real PostgreSQL upgrades; browser-check the integrated flow. Independent real-world evaluation is a separate accuracy exercise, not proof supplied by unit tests.
6. Phase 5: consolidate existing Trip Energy design with useful old overview depth after model/routing stability. Keep vehicle/specification/connector context, planned distance per day and date-based results without duplicate cards; show predicted versus observed, nominal/provisional/provenance, charging limitations, reserve and unavailable distinctly. Add an honest checkpoint-aware chart representation rather than drawing observation as charging. Preserve responsive drawers, both themes, accessibility and shared-view privacy. Current UI remains the foundation; broader changes are the friend's decision. Do not restore old calculations, missing-data zeroes or misleading mileage/range claims.
7. Release: validate all changed services, client build/types/lint and environment schema/capabilities; publish child dev/main before parent pins and root main only when ready to deploy. Follow docs/agents/release.md. Smoke-test real guest/authenticated flows and confirm workflow/health; use existing rollback process on failure. Do not assume pushing dev deployed it.

**Current mathematical baseline:** CONSUMPTION: E=d*c/100 kWh; SoC depletion=100*E/usableCapacity when usable capacity is known. RATED_RANGE: SoC depletion=100*d/ratedRange, provisional; direct consumption stays null. Optional kWh requires known usable capacity. Raw predicted arrival remains distinct from clamped display. An observed checkpoint changes onward state only, not earlier feasibility. Reserve is a separate threshold (current default 12%), not a 12% energy multiplier. Unknown uncertainty has no invented percentage. Constant-power charging is nominal, not a validated charge curve.

**Evidence and limits:** Prior integrated checks: 113 frontend tests, planner script, TypeScript/build; 166 trip tests, 12 gateway passed/1 skipped, 26 mobility passed/1 skipped. IAM released-equivalent tree previously 161 passed/2 disabled. Tests were not rerun for this documentation-only review. Browser acceptance, live migration inspection and empirical accuracy evaluation remain outstanding. Keep these qualifications in the proposal report; describe Phase 4/5 as planned work, not delivered/validated research results.

## 2026-09-24 - Codex - Phase 3.1 garage troubleshooting

**Standing user constraint:** Do not change existing UI without explicit user approval. Logic/API/state fixes are allowed; UI additions/adjustments only when expressly requested or necessary for a specifically requested new feature. Phase 3.1 changes no JSX, CSS, layout, controls or visuals.

**Diagnosis correcting the preceding audit:** The user already clicked Save repeatedly. The unsaved selection is a symptom of an unsupported backend command, not a missed click. `.env.local` directs the localhost frontend to the university VM. Local user-service commit `20a41c4` supports the energy commands but was never pushed/deployed. Its predecessor requires non-null catalogue consumption and has no energySelection field: catalogue add fails with the screenshot's 'must not be null', while update can return the unchanged legacy vehicle. The running public catalogue request through localhost returned 401 despite the local proxy's explicit anonymous GET allowance, consistent with older gateway/service security. No live authenticated account request was replayed or changed. The two vehicles are account-wide My Garage entries loaded for each trip, not hardcoded duplicates; do not delete or reset them.

**Minimal fix completed locally:** Client `d15dd47` verifies successful energy-command responses before mutation success can replace trip state, rejecting an ignored/mismatched model or consumption value even after HTTP 200. Legacy payload compatibility remains. Catalogue's obsolete null-consumption validation now reports the deployment mismatch rather than inventing consumption, and validation errors retain field names. Only `vehicle-api.ts` and `tests/garage/api.test.mjs` changed. Existing UI is unchanged; messages use existing error handling. No new migration, consumption-policy change, account mutation, Phase 4 work or backend workaround.

**Verified:** 68 frontend garage/energy/guest/filter tests pass, including new old-server no-op, catalogue rejection and correct range/custom acknowledgement regressions. Existing local user-service garage/API/security tests 39/39 pass (VehicleGarageTests 17, UserVehicleControllerTests 13, UserVehicleServiceTest 9), with config-server dependency disabled for isolated tests. ## 2026-09-22 - Codex - Additional approved Phase 3 battery-state requirements

**Scope:** Planning requirements only; pre-Phase-3 UI cleanup is complete. Preserve Nickname (Optional), Vehicle Default / NAVIO Estimate / Custom, authoritative direct consumption in kWh/100 km, RATED_RANGE without fabricated consumption, and immediate trip-only Starting Battery. This entry supersedes the temporary overlay limitation as the desired final behavior, not as a claim of implementation.

**A. Canonical trip starting SoC:** One initial SoC for the entire trip, applied only at the beginning of Day 1. Changing it updates calculations immediately. For saved trips persist it in trip-owned state and restore it on reopening; never mutate My Garage vehicle defaults. Guests follow the existing guest-trip persistence lifecycle without account vehicle writes. Propagate chronologically through stops and days; do not introduce daily starting SoCs without an actual trip event/checkpoint. The current in-memory per-vehicle overlay is transitional and must be replaced by canonical trip-owned state during Phase 3.

**B. Optional observed SoC checkpoints:** Each itinerary stop may carry an optional actual user-observed SoC in the inclusive range 0-100%. Keep predicted arrival and observed SoC distinct. No checkpoint means normal predicted propagation. A checkpoint becomes the authoritative departure basis from that stop forward without rewriting earlier legs or the predicted arrival at that stop. Example: predicted arrival at B is 72%, observed is 68%; retain both, then seed subsequent predictions with 68%. Multiple checkpoints apply chronologically, including across days. Removing a checkpoint recomputes normally from the preceding state/checkpoint. Persist observations with saved stops; support guest and signed-in trips. A checkpoint does not imply charging, change consumption, update garage defaults or enable automatic calibration/learning.

**Phase 3 persistence/API decision:** Include additive trip/stop persistence, request/response DTOs, validation, mapping, autosave/hydration and guest-state integration in Phase 3 itself. Without these, reopen behavior and a single canonical propagation path cannot be delivered. Use the existing trip/stop storage mechanism where suitable; determine exact fields/migration need by inspecting current trip schemas during Phase 3 planning. Do not write trip state to the user-management vehicle contract. Preserve older payloads/records with absent checkpoint fields; explicitly distinguish missing observations from valid 0%. Define a documented initial-state policy for older trips without silently treating a missing value as observed zero. Read database-change instructions before schema/entity work and run real PostgreSQL checks if a migration is needed.

**Canonical integration / ordering:** Establish trip initial state and stop observation contracts first, wire persistence/reload, then implement one chronological energy/SoC projection consuming those inputs. Predicted values remain derived and distinguishable from user observations; retain observation identity through the stop association so later validation/calibration can consume it without redesigning trip-state representation. Define checkpoint ordering relative to charging events explicitly in the Phase 3 plan; never infer charging from an observation. Any secondary Set battery level action should be minimal wiring only; no stop UI redesign or Phase 5 consolidation. Automatic calibration and prediction-error learning remain future work.

**Acceptance checks for Phase 3:** Immediate initial-SoC updates; saved-trip reopen restoration; no garage writes; guest lifecycle isolation; Day 1 initialization only and cross-day carry-forward; predicted/observed distinction; multiple checkpoints; earlier-leg invariance; checkpoint deletion/reset; 0/100 accepted and out-of-range rejected; absent versus zero; saved-stop round-trip and old payload compatibility. No implementation was performed in this documentation session. Full Phase 3 implementation remains a separate approval/work item.

Only a Keycloak operator assigns OWNER. **The existing production realm needs the manual setup in [owner-role.md](owner-role.md)** before anyone can hold it.

That includes 30 publication service tests, 5 summarizer tests, and 8 + 17 controller slices (these need the config server on 8888). `PostgresSchemaTests` passes on disposable Postgres (V1 to V13 plus `validate` plus the JPQL feed query). The listing CHECK constraint was exercised directly in psql. api-gateway: 10 tests, including a new shared-plans routing test. Client: `tsc`, scoped ESLint and `next build` are clean. Explore UI was checked in Chromium against a stub gateway in light, dark and 390px: search merge, keyboard open, no overflow, and unlisted/dead tokens refused on `/explore/shared`. **Not browser-checked:** the owner dialog's Explore toggle (needs auth and a backend).

**Backend** (`server/trip-planning-service` `dev`, untracked): `V13__trip_publication.sql` (renumbered from V11 on 2026-09-26: Raum-1's pushed `fix/trip-specific-garage` holds V11 and V12) (one row per trip, token nullable and cleared on revoke, `sanitizer_version` so a redaction fix dead-links stale snapshots rather than serving them), `TripPublication`, `TripPublicationRepository`, `PlanPublicationSanitizer`, `ShareTokenGenerator`, `TripPublicationService`, `TripPublicationController` (owner-only `GET`/`POST preview`/`PUT`/`DELETE` under `/v1/trips/{tripId}/publication`), `SharedPlanController` (`GET /v1/shared-plans/{token}`, the service's one anonymous route), plus a modified `GlobalExceptionHandler`. `server/api-gateway` permits `GET /v1/shared-plans/{token}` only; `server/configuration-server` routes `/v1/shared-plans/**` to trip-planning.

The snapshot is **frozen at publish time**, not projected on read: the owner's later edits stay private until they press Update. `SharedPlanContent` renders both the owner's preview and the recipient page, so the preview cannot drift from what is served.

Two things to know before touching it:

- `usePublishPlan` must **not** take the `planner-autosave-<tripId>` mutation scope. It calls the autosave flush, which calls the autosave mutation; sharing that scope makes each wait for the other.
- `expectedTripVersion` is `trip.version`, which the client already has as the planner snapshot's `version` (`PlannerService` returns `trip.getVersion()` there). `TripResponse` does not expose it.

**Not built:** replace link / token rotation, the allow-copy option (no `allow_copy` column in V13), independent copying, guest "Sign in to publish", moderation/reporting of Explore listings, and server-side Explore search. `docs/api/Navio Open API.yaml` is not updated (it is also missing the feed and PATCH routes).

**Next:** browser-verify against a running backend, with owner account A, unrelated account B and a signed-out browser. Include publish-with-listing, unlist, and stop-sharing, and confirm each one disappears from the Explore feed. The plan's must-pass list is still unexercised end to end. Then split the commits: the client tree also holds the admin-console work, and the gateway file holds the vehicle-models permit.

## Observability fix + tracing details (written 2026-09-23, released 2026-09-27)

Edits are in the working tree on root `dev`, **uncommitted and not deployed**. Root-repo files only â€” no submodule changes. They only take effect on the VM after a root `main` push runs the deploy workflow.

**Files:** `.deploy/nginx/navio.conf`, `.deploy/compose.production.yml`, `.deploy/.env.example`, `.deploy/observability/prometheus.yml`, `.deploy/observability/grafana/datasources.yml`, `grafana/provisioning/datasources/datasources.yml`, `grafana/dashboards/navio-overview.json`.

### 1. Grafana dashboard showed no data â€” root cause

`proxy_set_header` does not merge across nginx configuration levels: declaring *any* `proxy_set_header` inside a `location` discards every server-level one. The `/grafana/` location declared only `Upgrade` and `Connection`, so it silently dropped `Host` and all `X-Forwarded-*` and sent `Host: navio_grafana` upstream. Grafana's CSRF middleware compares the browser `Origin` against that `Host` and answers 403 to every non-GET request; panels query over `POST /api/ds/query`, so every panel failed while the GET-based template variables still resolved. Fix repeats all six inherited headers in the location.

`location /` (navio-web) had the identical defect and is fixed the same way. It was not visibly broken because Auth.js is configured with an explicit `AUTH_URL`, but Next.js also compares `Origin` against `Host` to guard Server Actions.

`GF_SERVER_ROOT_URL`'s compose *default* was `http://`, inconsistent with `GF_SECURITY_COOKIE_SECURE=true`; changed to `https://`. The VM's `.env` already sets the value, so this only affects the fallback.

### 2. Tracing turned on in production

`MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ZIPKIN_ENABLED` binds to **no Spring property** and was always a no-op â€” the real switch is `management.tracing.export.enabled`. What actually kept tracing off was `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY=0.0`.

Now: a `zipkin` service (`openzipkin/zipkin:3.5.1`, matching the dev stack), `STORAGE_TYPE: mem` with `MEM_MAX_SPANS=50000`, `mem_limit: 320m`, backend network only. Sampling is `${NAVIO_TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY:-0.1}` and `MANAGEMENT_TRACING_EXPORT_ZIPKIN_ENDPOINT` is set on the shared Java env anchor â€” verified to reach all seven Java services.

- **Traces are in-memory and lost on restart.** Persisting them needs Elasticsearch or Cassandra, which this VM has no memory budget for.
- **Zipkin is not proxied by NGINX.** Its UI has no authentication. Traces are read through Grafana Explore, which is behind a login.
- Zipkin has `healthcheck: disable: true` on purpose: `deploy.sh` runs `compose up --wait` over the whole stack and **rolls the release back** if any container is unhealthy. A trace backend must never fail an application release.
- Grafana gets a Zipkin datasource (traceâ†’logs/metrics, copied from the dev stack's working config) and a Loki `derivedFields` entry that turns the trace id in Spring Boot's `[app,traceId,spanId]` log prefix into a link into Zipkin. Both stacks' datasource files now match.

**Memory:** declared `mem_limit` across the stack is now ~8.3 GB. These are limits, not reservations, but check `free -h` on the VM before releasing.

### 3. Dashboard links removed

`grafana/dashboards/navio-overview.json` had two `http://localhost:` links (Prometheus targets, Zipkin UI) that only ever worked on a machine running the stack locally; neither port is exposed in production. Removed rather than replaced: the file is installed into **both** dev and production by `deploy.sh`, Grafana serves it at the root in dev but under `/grafana` in production, and dashboard link URLs are not sub-path aware, so no single hardcoded URL is correct in both. Traces are reached via Grafana's Explore nav, or by clicking the TraceID field on a log line.

### Not verified / left for the next agent

- Nothing was deployed or exercised against the live VM. YAML/JSON syntax validated locally only.
- The Zipkin `metrics_path: /prometheus` scrape job mirrors the dev stack's; the target was not confirmed UP against a running container.
- Latent trap, **not** fixed because it needs a `server` submodule change: the config server serves `management.tracing.sampling.probability: 1.0` hardcoded for api-gateway, discovery-server and trip-planning-service, and `${TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY:1.0}` â€” a *different* env name â€” for community and mobility. This is harmless today only because services use `spring.config.import: configserver:` (config-data mode), where config-server values rank **below** `systemEnvironment`, so the compose env var wins. Switching to legacy bootstrap mode would flip that and start sampling 100% of requests.

Unrelated dirty work was present at session start and was preserved untouched: `client` and `server` submodules (link-sharing / gateway edits), and the untracked `docs/agents/*-plan.md` and `docs/research/`. Do not fold those into an observability commit.

## Released 2026-09-22: EV planner rework (root `main` `28b6d7e`)

Client `main` `7dee312`, server `main` `cdcd736`, mobility `main` `eb72b37`. Includes everything in the section below. **Live:** deploy run 35700267438 succeeded (all jobs); after the deploy `/health` returned 200, `/v1/trips` 401, and `/` 200. The whole flow was never checked in the real planner with live routes.

- **Production bug found after release: "Plan charging stops" failed.** trip-planning built the mobility route from the day's block items only, ignoring the day's start and end anchors. A day with one place between its anchors was rejected with 422 ("could not be optimized"), and other days were optimized on a different route from the one the planner displays. **Fixed on `dev`, not deployed:** trip-planning `c6d5b8c` (resolves anchors like the client's `resolveDayAnchors`: own anchor, else the previous day's end; sends them as `<blockClientId>:start/:end` stops; the applier appends chargers planned before `:end`; limit of 25 stops; logs mobility's status and body on failure), server `dev` `1657da2`, client `dev` `a4ab4b1` (shows the 422 `error` detail). trip-planning tests: 48/50 pass with a local config server; the 2 full-context tests need Postgres on 5432 (Docker was not running). The earlier "temporarily unavailable" 503 (14:49:58) has no recorded cause; mobility logged nothing, so it was probably a silent mobility 400. The new logging will show it if it recurs.
- **Open question for the user:** the "Charge each stop to" slider (`chargeStopTargetPctAtom`) is almost meaningless now. The backend optimizer already tries every 5% target up to 92% and picks the fastest; the slider only adds one more candidate. The user was offered its removal and has not answered yet.

## Live in production (before this release)

Root `0a4acc4` (deploy run 34829001804, success).

- **Structured trip location.** Trips store `destinationCity`, `destinationRegion`, `destinationCountryCode`, and `destinationCountry`, resolved server-side from `destinationId` through mobility-and-ev-service. `displayName` is optional; responses include a derived `title` (name -> country -> city after this release). Clients send only `destinationId` (plus dates/name).
- Copying a premade plan resolves a place id from the destination name before creating the trip (`resolveDestinationPlaceId` in `client/app/feature/planner/_components/destination-api.ts`).
- Trip country backfill completed 2026-09-15 (backup on VM: `/home/adminnav/navio-maintenance/flags-2026-09-15/trip-before-backfill.sql`); zero null country codes at verification. The runner stays disabled by default.

## Shared agent context and CI

- `AGENTS.md` (committed; Codex loads it, Claude loads it via local `CLAUDE.md` = `@AGENTS.md`) and `docs/agents/*`. Every session must update this file and the session log.
- `PostgresSchemaTests` in all four JPA services plus the `verify-database-schema` job in `deploy-backend.yml` must pass before images build.

## Known gaps

- **Left uncommitted in the root repo on purpose (2026-09-23):** `docs/agents/admin-dashboard-plan.md`, `docs/agents/plan-link-sharing-plan.md`, `docs/research/` (including `ev-range-test-standards.md`, which the garage work cites), `.deploy/compose.production.yml`, `.deploy/nginx/navio.conf`, `.deploy/observability/*`, `grafana/dashboards/navio-overview.json`, and a dirty `server` submodule working tree. These belong to earlier Codex sessions; per AGENTS.md Â§1.3 they were not committed or discarded. Whoever owns them should commit them.
- `client/CLAUDE.md` is **not** in `client/.gitignore`, so it shows as untracked on every client session and must be excluded by hand. Adding it to `.gitignore` would remove the footgun.
- None of the released client UI work was checked end to end in a real browser session with the backend: theme in both modes, dashboard, delete trip, sidebar recent plan, charging slider thumb alignment and drag-and-drop, narrow widths.
- `components/theme/*` is committed but unused (color theme picker unwired).
- `components/profile/settings-sidebar.tsx` has `href="/dashboard"className=` with no space (compiles, cosmetic).
- ESLint warnings: unused `tripDateLabel` in `empty-itinerary-callout.tsx`, unused `RouteSegmentStatus` in `planner-map-google.tsx`.
- trip-planning-service `@SpringBootTest` and `@WebMvcTest` tests need the configuration server on port 8888; the two full-context tests also need Postgres on 5432 (no test `application.yml`).
- user-management-service full-context test is `@Disabled` (needs Keycloak); mobility and community default tests run on H2.
- Premade-plan copy resolves the destination by name; not tested in a browser yet.
- `docs/api/Navio Open API.yaml` trip schemas describe a planned design and do not match the current trip DTOs; `title` order change is not documented there.

## 2026-10-11 - Codex - Diagnose dashed Taoyuan MRT leg against running API

**User follow-up:** The purple line is visible; user correctly identified it as the Taoyuan Airport MRT and asked why no rail route is found.
**Verified cause:** Anonymous POST through the running localhost3000 /api/routes/directions proxy for Airport Terminal 2 (25.077,121.232) -> Taipei Main Station (25.046,121.517), with arrivalMode=transit and railOnly=true, still returns a ROUTED transit segment with a BUS ride and 4310 moving seconds. The newly implemented strict backend filter is not active in this running API path. The local client's guard rejects that bus and draws the observed straight-line dashed fallback. This is not proof the physical MRT line is absent. No account/trip changes or direct fresh Google request made; the upstream result may be cached.
**Schedule evidence:** Adapter still omits departureTime. Google documents that omission means current execution time. Probe was around 23:30 Bangkok on Oct 11 / 00:30 Taipei on Oct 12. Official A13 first/last guide lists A13 -> A1 express last 22:55 and commuter last 23:35: https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/dep-A13. Overnight timing is a plausible additional reason for a bus-first result, not a directly verified provider-ranking cause. User's actual itinerary date/time was not inspected. Background rail geometry remains visible independently of route availability.
**Changes/git:** Diagnosis only; no further application, scheduling, schema, source commit, push or deployment. All previous rail fixes/concurrent client work/logs/reports preserved. First root pull failed with Cannot fast-forward to multiple branches; notes were inadvertently committed on dev as c5a43ed before branch creation ran. Retried pull successfully, then cut docs/mrt-route-diagnosis-2026-10-11 from dev and committed this correction to both notes files, merging locally to dev only without rewriting history. Client remains feat/route-focus-by-place, mobility fix/station-rail-routes, server main; localhost3000 kept running.
**Next:** Run/deploy the already-tested rail-selecting backend with the client; avoid claiming the current running route finds MRT until this happens. For planned trips, implement destination-aware itinerary departure timestamps and include them in provider/cache keys, with an explicit policy for unscheduled or out-of-window dates. Do not force a purple road/straight-line result to masquerade as a rail route. Previous handoff remains applicable.

## 2026-10-11 - Codex - Rail-only station legs fixed locally

**Request/findings:** The user's Airport Terminal 2 -> Taipei Main Station leg displayed bus 1819 over the road while the purple Airport MRT was visible. They explicitly requested subway -> subway to use rail, never a bus. The former adapter sent unrestricted TRANSIT, no alternatives and no departureTime, then selected the first Google route. Google's allowedTravelModes is only a preference, so setting it alone would not satisfy the rule.
**Delivered, UNCOMMITTED:** Client rail detection in routes/leg-travel-mode.ts, derived optional railOnly on destination directions points in trip-route.helpers.ts/types.ts, and the restriction in the query signature. Typed rail stations and legacy Subway/MRT/BTS/Train labels qualify; generic transit stations and manual transit between other places retain bus support. Drive/Walk overrides still work. trip-route-query.ts applies enforceRailOnlyRoutes so an older backend cannot draw a bus on a rail leg; invalid results lose bus geometry/chips/time and become a straight-line fallback. route-segment-info.tsx shows No rail route. Mobility RoutePointRequest accepts nullable railOnly with backwards-compatible constructors. GoogleRoutesAdapter requests RAIL plus alternatives and selects a rail-only route with at least one rail ride, rejects every bus transfer/unknown vehicle/walking-only result, preserves provider geometry/line colors and returns an empty plan normally when no rail exists. RouteService separates rail/general cache entries and reports No rail route was found. No entity, migration, trip-persistence or dependency change.
**Verified:** Full client suite 224/224; final transit tests 17/17 after strengthening the response check against non-walk/non-transit steps. Final TypeScript, changed-source ESLint and whitespace checks pass. Full mobility suite 67 run: 66 passed, one PostgresSchemaTests skipped because no database URL was supplied (no schema change). Added adapter/service regressions and a real Jackson 3 RoutePointRequest JSON compatibility/round-trip test. Chrome exercised actual fetchTripRoutes/getTripRouteGroups, RouteSegmentInfo and shared map draw pieces with mocked API replies: railOnly request, purple MRT geometry/chip, bus-only/mixed/missing ride rejection, general buses, Drive override, dark 390px no overflow/no page errors. Screenshot inspected. Temporary app/rail-route-check-20261011 page removed; fixture/browser/results/screenshot at C:/Users/User/.codex/tmp/navio-station-rail-20261011/. Backend log: mobility target/rail-route-verification.log. No live Google request, real map geometry or production acceptance claimed.

## 2026-10-11 - Codex - Repeat-visit design recommendation (not implemented)

**User scenario:** Arrival at airport -> hotel check-in -> sightseeing -> return to the same hotel that evening. User asked whether Start/End destinations, map +N badges and an already-added indication should support this.
**Recommendation:** Use existing day boundaries for Start airport / End hotel, with hotel check-in as a normal intermediate stop. Also allow multiple regular visits to the same place within one day for returns that occur before its end. Each visit gets a unique item ID with separate time, notes, cost and visited status. Existing hotel End naturally becomes the next day's default Start/End through resolveDayAnchors.
**UI:** Replace the repeated-place disabled Added action with an existing-use indicator (e.g. Already in Day 1) and explicit Add another visit action. Show the chosen day and visit count, including relevant Start/End roles. Group regular stops together with anchors on both Google and Mapbox maps; a single pin shows the leading stop number and +N additional occurrences (+1 for two total), and an accessible popover lists/selects each visit or day-boundary role. Keep routing in itinerary order; map grouping must not deduplicate the route. Fresh visits should not inherit earlier cost/progress.
**Source findings:** selectedSearchPlaceIsAddedAtom and addSelectedPlaceToTripAtom in client trip-builder.atoms.ts currently reject same-place additions within a block. PlannerService.syncItems persists by item clientId, and V4 uniqueness is (block_id, client_id), not place_id. buildAnchorMarkers/AnchorMapPin already group day anchors, but regular place pins render separately. The expected work is primarily client-side; verify actual save/reload, routing/export and optimizer behavior before asserting full compatibility.
**Workspace:** Advice only; no source or database edits, tests or browser run. All previous uncommitted features/report/editor/log work preserved. Mandatory notes committed on root docs/repeat-visits-design-2026-10-11 from synced dev, merged locally to dev only; no push, submodule pins, main changes or deployment.

## 2026-10-11 - Claude - Production incident: planner snapshots unreadable; hotfix + TWD release

**Incident:** Release a9fc36f (first production release of Phase 4A + transit) broke every saved-trip load ("The saved trip could not be loaded. Please retry.", dashboard stats failing) and new trips got no days. Cause: trip-planning-service HTTP runs Jackson 3, but `energyState`, `observedSocCheckpoint` and `arrivalMode` are Jackson 2 `JsonNode`s, which Jackson 3 serialized as bean getters (`{"array":false,...,"nodeType":"NULL"}`); the client's schema rejected every snapshot. Introduced with Phase 4A energy fields, never previously released; earlier browser checks used mocked APIs, and slice tests never asserted these fields.
**Fix:** trip ca2befc adds `config/LegacyJsonNodeModule` (Jackson 3 module: writes Jackson 2 nodes as raw JSON; explicit null -> NullNode, omitted -> null). `PlannerJsonContractTests` drives real HTTP both ways and fails 3/3 without the module. Reproduced and verified against the released service locally (real Postgres): empty trip energyState null, save/reload of energy state, observation, placeType and arrivalMode; the client's real `getPlannerSnapshot` accepts the fixed payload and rejects the broken one.
**Also released at user request:** Codex's TWD currency (trip f777e73, client 306ee3e); budget_currency is varchar(3), no migration. Trip 199/199 on real Postgres, client 133/133 + TypeScript + lint.
**Git:** trip main/dev 77d21f3, client main/dev 306ee3e, server main/dev 5956733, root pins pushed to dev and main (deploys). User's checkouts were switched to main earlier this session; two stale 2026-09-26 release worktrees in %TEMP% holding main (only deleted tracked files, no work) were removed.
**Next:** After deploy, confirm a real signed-in trip loads and saves. Consider moving the trip DTOs to Jackson 3 types and removing the module. Phase 4A acceptance (optimize/apply/reopen) is still outstanding.

## 2026-10-13 - Codex - TipTap build failure diagnosed; no fix applied

**Diagnosis:** The reported Turbopack `Can't resolve '@tiptap/core'` error is local dependency-install drift after the latest client pull. Current `package.json` and `package-lock.json` correctly declare and lock TipTap `3.31.4`, including `@tiptap/core` and `@tiptap/pm`. The local `node_modules` tree has no `@tiptap` directory, `npm ls` reports the packages absent, and direct Node resolution returns `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. The installed-tree lock timestamp predates the pulled package files by roughly 17.5 hours, confirming dependencies were not synchronized after the pull.

**Scope:** Diagnosis only at the user's explicit request. No package installation, source edit, lockfile edit, build retry, or application fix was performed. The source import and committed dependency metadata are internally consistent. When authorized, refresh the local install from the committed lockfile, restart the dev process, and verify module resolution/build.
